# Architecture

deployarr installs Windows on bare-metal PCs from one central server. A technician types the Windows product key. When lookup cannot find the PC, the technician also picks a task sequence from a list, or types its order number when the SQL order lookup add-on is installed. It copies the parts of Microsoft Deployment Toolkit (MDT) that work and fixes the faults listed in [MDT 8456 reference](research/mdt-8456.md) and [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md). This page explains the design and why each choice was made. For the build order, see [Roadmap](roadmap.md).

## Requirements

These come from the product owner and drive every decision below.

- Zero-touch deployment, Windows first, except for the product key.
- A base program does the deployment and every standard task sequence feature. Optional features are add-ons that an admin installs separately, from an **Add add-on...** button in the web UI. See [Add-ons](#add-ons).
- Each PC has a Windows product key on a sticker. A technician types it during the deployment, on the same screen as the order number when lookup asks for one.
- Order lookup in an SQL database, as an optional add-on with high priority. It is the first add-on the owner named. The order's OS identifier selects the task sequence. A manual list of task sequences is the fallback.
- deployarr works with no order database. It then deploys from pre-staged records and the manual list.
- deployarr only reads the order database. It never writes to it, not even deployment status.
- A PC is matched in three ways, and all three must work: the order table holds the motherboard serial number, a technician scans or types the order number, or someone pre-stages the PC in the web UI. Pre-staging needs no order database. The first two ways come from the SQL order lookup add-on.
- The order database engine is not fixed. Support Microsoft SQL Server, PostgreSQL, and MySQL or MariaDB.
- Task sequences that work like MDT's: ordered groups and steps, conditions, variables, scripts, commands, and reboots.
- Every device has a working driver when the deployment finishes.
- Configurable applications, with a simple UI to manage drivers and software.
- A finished PC ends in one of two states, picked for each deployment. A PC that is ready to use skips OOBE and has a local admin account. A PC that is ready for the customer deploys in audit mode and then runs `sysprep /oobe /shutdown`, so the customer sees OOBE on first boot.
- PCs stay in a workgroup by default. Domain join is an option in the base program. Entra ID join and Autopilot are add-ons.
- Admins configure and run deployarr through a web UI. After the first release, a desktop program on the server shows the same UI in its own window.
- Install from an ISO or a WIM.
- A WinPE-based preinstall environment.
- PXE boot for UEFI with Secure Boot on and for legacy BIOS.
- PXE on one subnet, with as many PCs deploying at once as the server can serve. The design keeps routed subnets possible for a later release.
- One central deployment server that runs on Windows or Linux.

## Components

```
                 deployment server (Windows or Linux)
 +---------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  deployarr-server (one binary)                                      |
 |                                                                     |
 |  PXE: proxyDHCP (UDP 67, 4011), TFTP (UDP 69)                       |
 |  HTTPS API for agents and the web UI, content downloads             |
 |  lookup chain and deployment planning                               |
 |  add-on host (wasmtime) -> connections: order DBs, HTTPS APIs       |
 |  libraries: OS images, drivers, apps, task sequences, boot images   |
 |  storage: SQLite database + content store directory                 |
 +---------------------------------------------------------------------+
            ^ HTTPS, server certificate pinned by the agent
            |
 target PC: PXE -> WinPE (deployarr-agent is the shell)
            -> new Windows (deployarr-agent runs as a Windows service)
```

There are two programs. `deployarr-server` runs on the deployment server. `deployarr-agent` is one Windows executable that runs on the target PC, first as the WinPE shell and then as a Windows service in the new OS. Everything else is a library crate that these two programs share. Add-ons are WebAssembly components that the server runs. The agent never loads add-on code. See [Add-ons](#add-ons).

## Deployment flow

1. The PC PXE-boots. The proxyDHCP service reads the client architecture (DHCP option 93) and sends Microsoft-signed boot programs for that firmware type, with DHCP option 252 naming the BCD. See [PXE](#pxe).
2. The boot manager loads WinPE from the server over TFTP. WinPE starts `deployarr-agent` from `winpeshl.ini`.
3. The agent initializes WinPE (`wpeinit`), loads the boot image's network and storage drivers with `drvload`, and connects to the server over HTTPS.
4. The agent gathers facts and sends them to the server. Facts include the SMBIOS data (system, baseboard, and chassis serials, UUID, make, model, SKU, and chassis type), the form factor flags (laptop, desktop, and server), the virtual machine (VM) flag, the hypervisor vendor, MAC addresses, firmware type, Secure Boot state, TPM version, memory, processor cores, disks, and the hardware IDs and compatible IDs of every device. See [Chassis and VM facts](#chassis-and-vm-facts).
5. The server runs the lookup chain (see [Order lookup](#order-lookup)). The result is a task sequence and a set of variables, or a prompt for the technician, such as an add-on's order-number prompt or the manual list. If the task sequence activates Windows, the agent then asks for the product key. See [Activation](#activation).
6. The server pins the task sequence version. It asks each add-on to expand its steps into built-in steps, compiles the result, and sends the compiled program and variables to the agent. See [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable).
7. The agent runs the WinPE steps. A typical run checks readiness, partitions the disk, downloads and applies the OS image, injects drivers, writes the unattend file to `\Windows\Panther\unattend.xml`, runs `bcdboot`, and installs itself as a service in the offline image.
8. The PC reboots into Windows Setup. When Setup finishes, or reaches audit mode, the agent service resumes the sequence at the next step.
9. The agent installs applications and Windows updates, runs scripts, reboots as steps request, and checks that every device has a driver.
10. The agent turns off the built-in Administrator account, deletes every answer file that Windows Setup can find on the PC's disk, and reports success. It then uploads its logs, removes itself, and runs the finish action. A PC that is ready to use restarts or shuts down. A PC for the customer ends any running `sysprep.exe` and runs `sysprep /oobe /shutdown /quiet`. See [End state](#end-state).
11. The server marks the deployment complete. Later PXE boots of that PC fall through to the local disk.

## Core data shapes

The task sequence model lives in `deployarr-core`, a `no_std` crate with no I/O. See [Crate layout](#crate-layout). The server, the agent, and the web UI all use it. These sketches show the shape, not final code.

```rust
pub struct TaskSequence<A> {   // A is the action type that a step holds
	pub id: TaskSequenceId,   // upper-case, like "W11-PRO-STD"
	pub version: u32,         // every save creates a new version
	pub name: String,
	pub default_vars: DefaultVars,   // no two entries name the same variable
	pub root: Group<A>,
}

pub type AuthoredSequence = TaskSequence<Action>;   // what an admin edits and the server stores

pub enum Node<A> {
	Group(Group<A>),
	Step(Step<A>),
}

pub struct NodeMeta {
	pub id: NodeId,           // stable UUID. Survives a rename or a move
	pub name: String,
	pub enabled: bool,
	pub continue_on_error: bool,
	pub condition: Option<Condition>,
}

pub struct Group<A> {
	pub meta: NodeMeta,
	pub children: Vec<Node<A>>,
}

pub struct Step<A> {
	pub meta: NodeMeta,
	pub run_in: RunIn,        // WinPe, FullOs, or Either. Checked against the action at save
	pub action: A,
}

pub enum Action {   // what a stored step holds. See Task sequences stay loadable
	Builtin(BuiltinAction),   // written in JSON exactly as a built-in action
	AddOn(AddOnStep),
	ApplyJoinProfile { stage: JoinStage },   // OfflineImage or FullOs
}

pub enum BuiltinAction {   // everything the agent runs
	RunCommand { command: Template, working_dir: Option<Template>, exit: ExitCodes, limits: StepLimits, files: StepFiles },
	RunPowerShell { script: ContentRef, args: Vec<Template>, exit: ExitCodes, limits: StepLimits, files: StepFiles },
	SetVariable { name: SettableVarName, value: Template },   // the name cannot start with `_`
	Restart { into: RestartTarget },
	PartitionDisk { layout: DiskLayoutId, disk: DiskSelector },
	ApplyOsImage { image: OsImageRef },
	ApplyDrivers { selection: DriverSelection },
	ApplyUnattend { template: UnattendTemplateId },
	InstallApplications { apps: AppSelection },   // One(AppId) or FromList(VarName)
	InstallUpdates {              // full OS only. Windows Update or WSUS. See Windows Update
		source: UpdateSource,
		missing: MissingUpdatePolicy,
		max_passes: NonZeroU8,    // default 7
		exclude: Vec<UpdateExclusion>,   // default empty
		timeouts: UpdateTimeouts,
	},
	VerifyDrivers { policy: MissingDriverPolicy },
	EnableBitLocker { method: EncryptionMethod, full_disk: bool, wait_secs: Option<NonZeroU32>, tpm_failure: TpmFailure },   // full OS only
	SuspendBitLocker { restarts: SuspendRestarts },   // full OS only. Never decrypts
	CheckReadiness { checks: ReadinessChecks },   // Windows 11 minimums by default
	ActivateWindows { online: bool },   // full OS only. Installs the product key typed in WinPE
	ServerCall { send: Vec<VarName>, limits: StepLimits },   // add-on steps expand to it
	AddProvisioningPackage { package: SecretRef, limits: StepLimits },   // the one action that takes a secret
}

pub struct StepFile {   // a file the agent writes to the step's private folder
	pub name: FileName,
	pub content: ContentRef,   // content only, never a secret
}

pub struct ExitCodes {          // read with success() and reboot(). No code is in both lists
	success: NonEmpty<i32>,   // Default [0]
	reboot: Vec<i32>,         // success, then a reboot. Default [3010, 1641]
}

pub struct StepLimits {
	pub max_runtime_secs: Option<NonZeroU32>,   // whole seconds. Default none
	pub rerun_if_interrupted: bool,   // default false
}

pub enum Condition {
	All(NonEmpty<Condition>),
	Any(NonEmpty<Condition>),
	Not(Box<Condition>),
	Var { name: VarName, test: Test<NonEmptyTemplate> },
	Registry(RegistryCondition),   // a key, or a REG_DWORD, REG_QWORD, REG_SZ, or REG_EXPAND_SZ value
	File { path: Template, version: Option<Comparison<FileVersion>>, modified: Option<Comparison<Timestamp>> },
	Folder { path: Template, modified: Option<Comparison<Timestamp>> },
	InstalledMsi(MsiProduct),   // ProductCode or UpgradeCode. Full OS only
	Wmi { namespace: String, query: Template, ignore_failure: bool },   // full OS only
}

pub struct RegistryCondition {   // read with key(), value(), value_type(), and test(). A test that takes data, or a value type, needs a value
	key: Template,
	value: Option<RegValueName>,   // none tests the key
	value_type: Option<RegType>,   // the type the value must have. None takes any type
	test: Test<RegData>,
}

// An operator with its operand. Exists and NotExists take none, and every other operator takes one
pub enum Test<T> { Exists, NotExists, Equals(T), NotEquals(T), Less(T), LessEqual(T), Greater(T), GreaterEqual(T), Like(T) }

pub enum CompareOp { Equals, NotEquals, Less, LessEqual, Greater, GreaterEqual }   // for file versions and timestamps
```

Each MDT step type that does real work becomes a typed `BuiltinAction` variant with typed parameters. `RunCommand` and `RunPowerShell` cover everything else. MDT passes every step's settings as untyped variables and command lines, so a typo becomes a runtime failure. With typed actions, the editor and the server reject a bad step when you save it.

MDT's editor saves most of a step's settings in the step's `<defaultVarList>` ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#task-sequence-editor-model)). The MDT importer, `deployarr.mdt-import` (see [Planned add-ons](#planned-add-ons)), treats those entries by how it converts the step. For a step that becomes a typed action, the entries fill the action's parameters, so `DriverInjectionMode` `AUTO` becomes the `ApplyDrivers` selection `Auto` (see [Selection](#selection)). For a step that it imports as a placeholder, it drops the entries and adds a note that names each variable. Entries never become the sequence's `default_vars`, and the model has no step-scoped variables. MDT applies a step's list before it evaluates the step's condition, and it clears the list when the step ends. A lookup reads a step's value only when the global value is empty ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#variables)). As sequence-wide `default_vars`, the entries would leak into later steps and their conditions. The importer rules in this paragraph are triage's recommendation, which the owner accepted on [#181](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/181#issuecomment-6961360165).

The owner later decided what a note holds and what happens to an entry that matches no parameter ([#181](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/181#issuecomment-6966808671)). The MDT paths in this list are relative to `Source/MDT.8456` in the [MDT 8456 sources](research/mdt-8456.md#sources).

- When the importer converts a step to a typed action, it drops each entry that matches no parameter of that action and names it in a note on the converted step, as a placeholder's note does. Restart computer's `Message` and `MessageTimeout` match no parameter of `Restart`, and Install Applications' `ApplicationSuccessCodes` matches no parameter of `InstallApplications` (`Templates/Client.xml:345-352, 387-393`).
- A note names each dropped variable and holds no values. A value can be a secret. MDT's editor saves a Run Command Line step's run-as password in plain text as `SMSTSRunCommandLineUserPassword` ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#task-sequence-editor-model)), and MDT's `CaptureOnly.xml` template holds that variable, with an empty value, in a step's `<defaultVarList>` (`Templates/CaptureOnly.xml:43-51`).

The condition types match what MDT's engine evaluates. MDT also has make, model, location, and computer conditions, which compare by substring, so a gateway of `10.0.0.1` also matches `10.0.0.10`. deployarr expresses those as `Var` conditions on gathered facts, which compare exactly.

Add-ons extend this model in three ways. A stored step can hold a step from an add-on. The `ApplyJoinProfile` join slot marks where the deployment's join profile adds its steps. The agent's actions gain `ServerCall`, `AddProvisioningPackage`, and step files. The task sequence types take the action type as a parameter, and the program holds only built-in actions. [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable) shows these shapes.

### Parameter types

```rust
pub struct NodeId(pub Uuid);
pub struct Template(String);              // the text as written. Substitution happens at run time
pub struct NonEmptyTemplate(Template);    // a Template that is not empty
pub struct SettableVarName(VarName);      // a VarName that does not start with `_`
pub struct VarAssignment { pub name: SettableVarName, pub value: Template }
pub struct DefaultVars(Vec<VarAssignment>);   // read with as_slice(). No two names are equal in any case
pub struct StepFiles(Vec<StepFile>);      // read with as_slice(). No two names are equal in any case
pub struct FileName(String);              // one Windows file name, never a path
pub struct SecretRef { pub source: SecretSource, pub field: FieldKey }   // a secret file field of the add-on that expanded the step
pub struct JoinPasswordRef(String);       // names a Domain join profile's password, which the server stores
pub struct NonEmpty<T>(Vec<T>);           // a list with at least one item

pub enum RunIn { WinPe, FullOs, Either }
pub enum RestartTarget { WinPe, InstalledOs }
pub enum DiskSelector { FirstInternal }   // the default
pub enum DriverSelection { Auto, Profile, ProfileAll }
pub enum MissingDriverPolicy { Fail, Warn, Ignore }
pub enum MissingUpdatePolicy { Fail, Warn, Ignore }      // default Warn
pub enum UpdateSource { WindowsUpdate, Wsus(WsusUrl) }   // default WindowsUpdate
pub enum UpdateExclusion { Kb(NonZeroU32), UpdateId(Uuid) }
pub enum AppSelection { One(AppId), FromList(VarName) }
pub enum SecretSource { StepConfig, JoinProfile, AddOnSettings }   // AddOnSettings is `addon_settings` in JSON

pub struct ReadinessChecks {
	pub memory: MemoryCheck,
	pub disk: DiskCheck,
	pub processor: ProcessorCheck,
	pub uefi: CheckMode,             // default Fail
	pub tpm_2_0: CheckMode,          // default Fail
	pub secure_boot: CheckMode,      // default Warn
}

pub struct MemoryCheck { pub min_mib: u32, pub mode: CheckMode }        // mebibytes. Default 4096, Fail
pub struct DiskCheck { pub min_gb: u32, pub mode: CheckMode }           // decimal gigabytes. Default 64, Fail
pub struct ProcessorCheck { pub min_cores: u32, pub mode: CheckMode }   // processor cores. Default 2, Fail

pub enum CheckMode { Fail, Warn, Off }

pub enum EncryptionMethod { OsDefault, Aes128, Aes256, XtsAes128, XtsAes256 }   // default OsDefault
pub enum TpmFailure { Fail, Skip }   // default Fail
pub struct SuspendRestarts(u8);      // 1 to 15. Default 1

pub struct UpdateTimeouts {          // whole seconds, for one phase of one pass
	pub search_secs: NonZeroU32,     // default 3600
	pub download_secs: NonZeroU32,   // default 7200
	pub install_secs: NonZeroU32,    // default 7200
}

pub struct Comparison<T> { pub op: CompareOp, pub value: T }
pub struct FileVersion { pub major: u16, pub minor: u16, pub build: u16, pub revision: u16 }
pub struct Timestamp(pub OffsetDateTime);
pub enum MsiProduct { ProductCode(Uuid), UpgradeCode(Uuid) }
pub struct RegValueName(String);          // a registry value name that is not empty
pub enum RegType { None, Sz, ExpandSz, Binary, Dword, MultiSz, Qword }   // REG_NONE to REG_QWORD
pub enum RegData { String(RegText), Integer(u64) }   // compared by the `Var` rules
pub struct RegText(String);               // registry data as text that is not empty
```

- `NodeId` wraps a UUID. The core crate never generates a random ID, so it leaves out the `uuid` crate's `v4` feature. On `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `uuid` stops a build that has `v4` unless the build also turns on one of its randomness features: `js`, `rng-getrandom`, or `rng-rand`. `plan` derives the IDs of the steps of each expansion with the `v5` feature, which needs no randomness. See [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable).
- `TaskSequenceId` holds one or more of `A` to `Z`, digits, `_`, or `-`.
- `VarName` holds 1 to 256 ASCII letters, digits, `_`, or `-`. Two names that differ only in case are equal. The name keeps the spelling its author wrote. The length, the character classes, and the case rule are MDT's. The ASCII limit is deployarr's choice. See [Variables](#variables).
- `SettableVarName` is a `VarName` that does not start with `_`. `SetVariable` and the entries of `default_vars` take it, so a sequence cannot set a name that belongs to the agent. Conditions and `FromList` take a `VarName`, so they can still read one.
- `StepFiles` keeps its list private, and `StepFiles::new` rejects two files whose names are equal in any case, because Windows does not assume that names differ by case alone ([Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/naming-a-file)). The JSON is a plain array of files.
- `DefaultVars` keeps its list private, and `DefaultVars::new` rejects two entries whose names are equal in any case. Code that builds a sequence, such as an importer or the editor, therefore cannot write a `default_vars` list that the parser would reject. The JSON is a plain array of entries.
- `NonEmpty<T>` holds at least one item. `All`, `Any`, and the success list of `ExitCodes` use it, so none of them can be empty.
- `DiskLayoutId`, `OsImageRef`, `UnattendTemplateId`, `ContentRef`, `AppId`, and `JoinPasswordRef` are strings that reject an empty value.
- A `SecretRef` names a secret file field of the add-on that expanded the step, never the secret's value. The server holds the secret. In JSON it is an object such as `{"source": "step_config", "field": "package"}`. `source` is `step_config`, `join_profile`, or `addon_settings`, and `field` is a form field key (see [Configuration, secrets, and data](#configuration-secrets-and-data)). The owner chose this shape on [#189](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/189#issuecomment-6961472433).
- A `SecretRef` holds no add-on ID. The server finds the add-on and the config from the deployment and the node, as it does for `ServerCall`, so a reference cannot name another add-on's field. `plan` checks that the source fits the step that the expansion replaces. See [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable).
- The parser rejects a `SecretRef` with an unknown key, an unknown source such as `add_on_settings`, or an empty field key. It also rejects a bare string, the shape that `SecretRef` had before [#189](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/189). Each error gives the full path, such as `root.children[1].step.action.add_provisioning_package.package.source`.
- A `JoinPasswordRef` names the join account's password in a `Domain` join profile, a secret that the server stores with the profile. A `Domain` profile belongs to the base, not to an add-on, so its password gets its own type and stays a non-empty string in JSON (owner decision on [#189](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/189#issuecomment-6961472433)). See [Directory join](#directory-join).
- A step file's `FileName` holds 1 to 255 ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, or `-`, and does not end with `.`. The part before its first `.` is not a name that Windows reserves for a device, such as `NUL`, in any case. Windows treats `NUL.txt` as `NUL` and advises against a name that ends with a period ([Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/naming-a-file)). A step file's name is therefore one file in the step folder and never a path.
- `DiskSelector` has one rule so far, the first internal, non-removable disk.
- `MemoryCheck`, `DiskCheck`, and `ProcessorCheck` each hold a minimum and a `mode`, so the JSON for one is an object such as `{"min_mib": 4096, "mode": "fail"}`. The `uefi`, `tpm_2_0`, and `secure_boot` checks have no minimum, so each is a bare `CheckMode`, such as `"warn"`.
- `min_mib` is in mebibytes of 2^20 bytes, so the default 4096 is 4 GiB. `min_gb` is in decimal gigabytes of 10^9 bytes, the unit that disks are sold in, because a disk sold as 64 GB holds less than 64 GiB. `min_cores` counts processor cores.
- `max_runtime_secs` is a whole number of seconds and is never zero, because a zero limit fails every run. An earlier sketch had `max_runtime: Option<Duration>`. serde writes a `Duration` as an object with `secs` and `nanos`. deployarr limits a step in whole seconds, so the limit is one JSON integer and the field name carries the unit.
- `wait_secs` in `EnableBitLocker` follows the same rule. `SuspendRestarts` keeps its count private, and `SuspendRestarts::new` rejects a count outside 1 to 15. Its JSON is a bare integer. [BitLocker](#bitlocker) gives the meaning of each setting.
- `UpdateTimeouts` follows the same rules as `max_runtime_secs`. Each limit is whole seconds and never zero, and `max_passes` is never zero either. See [Windows Update](#windows-update).
- `WsusUrl` is an `http` or `https` URL, such as `https://wsus.example.com:8531`. The parser rejects an empty value, any other scheme, and a URL with user information such as `user:pass@`, so a credential never reaches the registry or a log. `UpdateExclusion::Kb` holds a KB article number without the `KB` prefix, and `UpdateId` holds an update ID, written as a GUID without braces.
- The `WsusUrl` parser reads the authority from after `//` up to the first `/`, `?`, or `#` ([RFC 3986, section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.2)).
- The owner decided that the parser rejects an `@` anywhere in a `WsusUrl` ([#173](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/173#issuecomment-6964618653)). An `@` in the authority ends user information ([RFC 3986, section 3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.2.1)). A user name or password with an unencoded `#`, `/`, or `?` ends the authority early, so its `@` falls after the authority. The rule still rejects that URL, as in `https://admin:123#x@wsus.example.com`. The rule is stricter than RFC 3986, which allows an `@` in a path, a query, or a fragment ([RFC 3986, section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.3), [section 3.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.4), [section 3.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.5)).
- The parser rejects a `WsusUrl` with no host. An `http` or `https` URI must name one ([RFC 9110, section 4.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-4.2.1), [section 4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-4.2.2)).
- The parser rejects a `[` without its `]`, a bracket anywhere else in the URL, and text after the `]` other than a `:` and a port. Brackets appear only around an IP literal host ([RFC 3986, section 3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.2.2)).
- The parser rejects an IP literal that is not an IPv6 address, such as `https://[hello]:8531`. RFC 3986 also allows a future format that starts with a version flag, such as `[v1.fe]`. It advises an application that does not know the version flag to return an error (the same section). deployarr knows no version flag, so the parser rejects that format too.
- The parser rejects a port that holds anything but digits. A port is a decimal number ([RFC 3986, section 3.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.2.3)).
- The parser rejects a port above 65535. An `http` or `https` port is a TCP port ([RFC 9110, section 4.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-4.2.1), [section 4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-4.2.2)). A TCP port is 16 bits ([RFC 9293, section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9293.html#section-3.1)).
- An empty port after the `:` means the scheme's default port (the same RFC 9110 sections), so the parser accepts it.
- The parser rejects a second `#`, as in `https://wsus.example.com/#a#b`. The first `#` starts the fragment, and a fragment holds only path characters, `/`, and `?` ([RFC 3986, section 3.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.5)). No path character is a `#` ([section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.3)).
- The parser accepts only ASCII letters, digits, and the symbols `-._~:/?#[]@!$&'()*+,;=%`. A URI holds only those characters ([RFC 3986, section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-2), [section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-2.2), [section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-2.3)). The parser therefore rejects a space, a control character, a character outside ASCII, and the symbols `"`, `<`, `>`, `\`, `^`, `` ` ``, `{`, `|`, and `}`. A URL written with a Windows backslash, as in `https://wsus.example.com\path`, fails.
- The parser rejects a `%` without two hex digits after it. A `%` in a URI starts a percent-encoded byte ([RFC 3986, section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-2.1)).
- The parser accepts the scheme in any case, as [RFC 3986, section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986.html#section-3.1) asks. The owner decided that the serializer normalizes only the scheme ([#173](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/173#issuecomment-6964618653)). It writes the scheme in lower case, its canonical form. It keeps the host, the port, the path, and the rest of the URL as the author wrote them.
- No `WsusUrl` error repeats the text. No error at `source` repeats a string written there either. serde's errors repeat an unknown source, such as a URL in place of `{"wsus": ...}`, and a value after `windows_update`, so the parser writes its own errors for both.
- `ExitCodes` holds `i32` values. Windows returns a process's exit code as a 32-bit `DWORD` ([GetExitCodeProcess](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-getexitcodeprocess)), and an `i32` holds those 32 bits. An exit code at or above `0x80000000` is written as its signed value, so `0x80070005` is `-2147024891`. The unsigned spelling fails to parse. An HRESULT is a `LONG`, a 32-bit signed integer ([Windows Data Types](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winprog/windows-data-types)), so the signed value is also the HRESULT's own value.
- `ExitCodes` keeps its fields private, and `ExitCodes::new` rejects a code that is in both `success` and `reboot`. A code means success, or success and then a reboot, never both.
- `Test<T>` carries an operator together with its operand. `Exists` and `NotExists` take no operand, and every other operator takes one, so a `Var` or `Registry` condition can neither lack the operand it needs nor carry one that it ignores. A `Var` condition compares against a `NonEmptyTemplate`, because `NotExists` is the test for an empty variable.
- `RegistryCondition` keeps its fields private, and `RegistryCondition::new` rejects a test other than `Exists` or `NotExists` that names no value, because a key has no data to compare. With no value name, `Exists` and `NotExists` test the key. `RegValueName` and `RegText` reject empty text, so a `Registry` condition never names an empty value and never compares against empty text. Code that builds a sequence therefore cannot write a `registry` condition that the parser would reject.
- `RegistryCondition::new` also rejects a value type on a condition that names no value, because a key has no type. Its message says that a value type needs a value name. When the test also takes data, `new` returns the comparison's error instead. MDT ignores `Type` on a test of the key, so this loses nothing that MDT can express (see [Conditions](#conditions)).
- `RegType` names the type that a `Registry` condition's value must have. Its seven variants, `None`, `Sz`, `ExpandSz`, `Binary`, `Dword`, `MultiSz`, and `Qword`, stand for `REG_NONE`, `REG_SZ`, `REG_EXPAND_SZ`, `REG_BINARY`, `REG_DWORD`, `REG_MULTI_SZ`, and `REG_QWORD`, the seven types that MDT maps a condition's `Type` to (`TsCore!0x1801ab134`). Their type numbers are 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 7, and 11 ([MS-RPRN Registry Type Values](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-rprn/25cce700-7fcf-4bb6-a2f3-0f6d08430a55)). MDT's mapping returns the same numbers (`TsCore!0x1801ab134`). The owner chose the optional value type on [#277](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/277#issuecomment-6988081213). [Conditions](#conditions) gives how the evaluator uses it.
- `Comparison<T>` takes only the six operators of `CompareOp`, so a file version or timestamp comparison cannot use `Like`, `Exists`, or `NotExists`.
- `Comparison<T>` has named `op` and `value` fields. An earlier sketch had the tuple `(CompareOp, T)`. serde writes a tuple as a JSON array, so a parse error would point at `version[1]`. With named fields it points at `version.value`.
- `FileVersion` is written as `a.b.c.d` and orders by its four fields.
- `Timestamp` is written in RFC 3339, such as `2026-01-31T08:00:00Z`. An earlier sketch had `DateTime` and named no format. RFC 3339 requires a UTC offset, so every value names one instant.
- The core crate parses a `Timestamp` with the `time` crate and writes the text itself, because `time`'s formatter needs `std`. It writes the same text that `time` writes. A fraction of a second loses its trailing zeros, and a zero offset is `Z`.
- `MsiProduct` is written as a GUID without braces. `RegData` is written as a bare JSON string or integer.
- A `Registry` condition reads `REG_DWORD`, `REG_QWORD`, `REG_SZ`, and `REG_EXPAND_SZ` values. `REG_DWORD` is a 32-bit number and `REG_QWORD` a 64-bit number ([Registry value types](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/sysinfo/registry-value-types)), and Windows declares `DWORD` and `QWORD` as unsigned ([Windows Data Types](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winprog/windows-data-types)). `RegData::Integer` therefore holds a `u64`, which holds every value of both types, and a negative number fails to parse.

### JSON format

A task sequence is stored and sent as one JSON document. `TaskSequence::from_json` parses it into the types above.

- The top-level `schema_version` is 1. The parser rejects any other version.
- Field names and variant names are in snake case, such as `continue_on_error` and `win_pe`. `RunPowerShell` is written as `run_powershell`, `EnableBitLocker` as `enable_bitlocker`, `SuspendBitLocker` as `suspend_bitlocker`, and the secret source `AddOnSettings` as `addon_settings`. A `RegType` is written as the snake case of its Windows name: `reg_none`, `reg_sz`, `reg_expand_sz`, `reg_binary`, `reg_dword`, `reg_multi_sz`, or `reg_qword` (owner decision on [#277](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/277#issuecomment-6989845415)). The parser takes no other spelling, so MDT's `REG_SZ` fails.
- A variant with data is an object with one key, the variant's name, such as `{"run_command": {"command": "ipconfig"}}`. A variant without data is a string, such as `"win_pe"`. Each node in `children` is `{"group": ...}` or `{"step": ...}`. This is serde's externally tagged form. The other choice puts a `"type"` key inside the object. serde buffers such an object before it reads the variant, so the path of a parse error stops at `action` and does not reach the field that failed.
- A field that the document omits takes its default when it has one above: `exit`, `limits`, `disk`, `checks`, every field of `install_updates`, `method`, `full_disk`, `tpm_failure`, `restarts`, and each field inside them. An omitted `files` list of a `RunCommand` or `RunPowerShell` action is empty (design decision), so a sequence written before step files existed still parses, and a command with no file needs no list. An omitted `Option` field is none. Every other field is required.
- An unknown field is an error, so a misspelled field name never passes as a default.
- The serializer writes every field, defaults included. A stored sequence then keeps its meaning if a default changes later.
- A parse error gives the path of the field that failed, such as `root.children[0].step.action.run_command.exit.success[1]`, and the reason.
- A value that is valid but not in canonical form still parses, and the serializer writes it back in canonical form. The parser accepts a GUID only as 32 hex digits in groups of 8-4-4-4-12 separated by hyphens, in any letter case, with or without one pair of braces. The serializer writes it in lower case without braces. The parser rejects every other GUID form, such as the URN form `urn:uuid:e2b6a5f1-4c3d-4b2a-9f8e-7d6c5b4a3f21` and the 32 hex digits without hyphens, though the `uuid` crate's own parser accepts both. The owner chose this, because the accepted forms are easy to widen later and hard to narrow once imported JSON relies on them ([#235](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/235#issuecomment-6981770390)). Node IDs, `MsiProduct` codes, and update IDs all parse through this one rule. A `WsusUrl` whose scheme is in upper case, such as `HTTPS://`, is written with `https://`. A file version part with leading zeros, such as `010`, is written as `10`. A timestamp with a lower-case `t` or `z`, a space in place of `T`, or a `+00:00` offset is written with `T` and `Z`. Any other offset is kept. A `Var` or `Registry` test of `{"exists": null}` is written as `"exists"`, and `{"not_exists": null}` as `"not_exists"`.
- A `Var` or `Registry` condition writes its operator and operand under `test`, by the variant rule above. `"test": "exists"` takes no operand, and `"test": {"greater_equal": 26100}` takes one. A file version or timestamp comparison keeps the `op` and `value` fields.
- A `registry` condition's value type is its `value_type` field. A condition with no value type leaves the field out or sets it to `null`, so a sequence written before the field existed still parses, and `schema_version` stays 1. The serializer writes `value_type` on every `registry` condition, as `null` when there is none, as it writes `value`.
- The parser also rejects the states below. Each error path names the field that holds the state:
  - An empty success list, at `exit.success`. MDT's engine has no default, so an empty `successCodeList` fails every exit code, including 0 ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#instruction-table-and-failure-handling)).
  - A code in both `success` and `reboot`, at the code in the list that comes second, such as `exit.reboot[0]`. An omitted list takes its default first. `{"success": [0, 3010]}` with no `reboot` therefore fails at `exit`, because the default `reboot` holds 3010.
  - An empty `All` or `Any`, at `all` or `any`. MDT treats an empty OR as true, which is rarely what the author meant ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)).
  - An operand on `exists` or `not_exists`, a missing operand on any other operator, or an empty `Var` operand, at the operator, such as `test.exists`.
  - `Like`, `Exists`, or `NotExists` in a file version or timestamp comparison, at `op`.
  - A negative registry integer, at the operator that holds it.
  - A `registry` test other than `exists` or `not_exists` that names no value, because a key has no data to compare. With `value` left out, the error is at `registry`, as a clash with the default of an omitted `reboot` list fails at `exit`. With `"value": null`, it is at `registry.value` or at the operator under `registry.test`, whichever the document holds second, as a code in both exit lists fails at the second one. MDT fails a comparison without a value name when it runs (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`).
  - A `value_type` on a `registry` condition that names no value, because a key has no type. The error paths follow the rule for a comparison that names no value. With `value` left out, the error is at `registry`. With `"value": null`, it is at `registry.value` or at `registry.value_type`, whichever the document holds second. The message says that a value type needs a value name. When the test also takes data, the parser reports the problem that it finds first, and the comparison when it finds both at once, as when `value` is left out or comes last.
  - A `value_type` that is not one of the seven names, such as `reg_link`, at `registry.value_type`.
  - A `value` of `""`, at `registry.value`, for every test. A test of the key leaves out `value` or sets it to `null`. MDT reads an empty value name as no value name, so its `Exists` tests the key and its comparisons fail (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`).
  - String registry data of `""`, at the operator that holds it, such as `registry.test.equals`. This covers `like` too, as for a `Var` operand. MDT fails a comparison against empty data when it runs, and checks only the six comparisons (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`).
  - A `SuspendBitLocker` count outside 1 to 15, at `restarts`.
  - A `SetVariable` step or a `default_vars` entry that names a variable that starts with `_`, at its `name`.
  - Two `default_vars` entries that name the same variable, in any case, at the second entry's name, such as `default_vars[1].name`.
  - Two step files with the same name, in any case, at the second file's name, such as `files[1].name`.
- The compiler rejects a `run_in` that the action cannot honor when the sequence is saved, so that the error names the node. See [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program).
- The parser reads a join slot whose `run_in` does not fit its stage, and a `ServerCall` or `AddProvisioningPackage` step outside an add-on's expansion. `validate` rejects both when the sequence is saved, and `plan` rejects both when a deployment is planned. See [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable).

`crates/deployarr-core/tests/data/windows-11-pro.json` is a sample that uses every condition, and every action that `deployarr-core` implements but the ones that come from add-ons: the add-on step, the join slot, `ServerCall`, and `AddProvisioningPackage`.

## Task sequence engine

### Compile the tree into a flat program

The server compiles a task sequence into a flat list of instructions before it sends it to the agent. The `plan` module in `deployarr-core` does it, and the `program` module holds the result:

```rust
pub fn plan(sequence: &AuthoredSequence, expansions: &Expansions) -> Result<Program, PlanErrors>;

pub struct Program {           // only plan builds one
	source: (TaskSequenceId, u32),
	instrs: Vec<Instr>,
}

pub struct Pc(pub usize);      // an index into instrs. instrs.len() is the end of the program

pub enum Instr {
	// If the condition is false, jump to `skip_to`, past the group's last child.
	// If it fails to evaluate, the group fails, and the run follows `on_error`.
	Enter { node: NodeId, condition: Option<Condition>, skip_to: Pc, on_error: OnError },
	// If the condition is false, skip the step.
	Exec { node: NodeId, condition: Option<Condition>, run_in: RunIn, action: BuiltinAction, on_error: OnError },
}

pub enum OnError {
	Continue,        // the step has continue_on_error. Never on an Enter
	JumpTo(Pc),      // the end of the nearest enclosing group with continue_on_error. For an Enter, it can be the group itself
	FailDeployment,  // no enclosing group has continue_on_error. For an Enter, the group itself has none either
}
```

The compiler drops disabled nodes and works out `on_error` for each step and each group, so a failure needs no group stack at run time. MDT's engine keeps disabled entries in its table and tracks failures with a stack of open groups, which it must save and rebuild on every resume (`_SMSTSInstructionStackString`). Working out `on_error` ahead of time removes that state.

The `on_error` of a group's `Enter` is the one that a step without `continue_on_error` directly inside the group gets. It is `JumpTo` the group's own `skip_to` when the group has `continue_on_error`. Otherwise it is the target that the group's parent passes down to its steps, or `FailDeployment` for the root. The run follows it when the group's condition fails to evaluate (see [Conditions](#conditions)). It is never `Continue`, which would run the group's children as if the condition were true.

A group, the root included, compiles to an `Enter` followed by its children. Each `Exec` carries the step's condition, `run_in`, and action, so the agent runs the program without the tree. An earlier sketch gave `Exec` only the node ID and `on_error`. Every jump lands after the instruction that holds it and no later than the end of the program, so a run moves only forward.

Like MDT, deployarr checks a group's condition only when it reaches the group, and resumes from a saved instruction pointer. A variable that a step sets in WinPE can therefore enable a group that runs after a reboot. That is how MDT's phase groups work.

The compiler also rejects these mistakes when you save, which MDT finds only at run time or never:

- A step whose `run_in` its action does not allow, by the table below, such as `ApplyOsImage` in the full OS. MDT fails such a step with error 50 when the engine reaches it.
- A step with `run_in` `WinPe` whose condition holds an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition at any depth. Both are full OS only. See [Core data shapes](#core-data-shapes).
- A group, the root included, or a step with `run_in` `Either`, whose condition holds an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition at any depth, and that comes at or before the first `Restart` with `into` `InstalledOs` in program order. Every run starts in WinPE (see [Deployment flow](#deployment-flow)). A run moves only forward, and only a `Restart` into `InstalledOs` leaves WinPE. So the run reaches every node before the first such `Restart` in WinPE, where an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition fails to evaluate on every run. The same holds for that `Restart` itself, because the run evaluates its condition before it restarts (owner decision on [#160](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/160#issuecomment-6981334349)). The `Restart` counts even when it has a condition, because no node before it can be reached in the full OS. A sequence with no such `Restart` therefore fails for every such group and step. A node after it passes. The run can still reach that node in WinPE, such as after a `Restart` into `WinPe`, and [Conditions](#conditions) says what happens then. A built-in step with `run_in` `FullOs` is not checked. The group that `plan` builds for an add-on step or a join slot keeps the step's condition, so the rule covers that group too, unless the step has `run_in` `WinPe`. A `WinPe` add-on step or join slot with an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition already breaks the rule on `WinPe` conditions. The rule skips it, so the step gets one error for its condition. At save, `validate` does not report this rule at a node after the first add-on step or join slot that the program keeps, because its expansion might hold the `Restart` (owner decision on [#160](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/160#issuecomment-6981685250)). The owner chose this rule on [#160](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/160#issuecomment-6961338417).
- A `Restart` step with `run_in` `WinPe` that comes before the first `PartitionDisk` step in program order. Until that step, the state lives on `X:`, and a reboot loses it, so the deployment starts over from the first step. See [State on disk and resume](#state-on-disk-and-resume). A sequence with no `PartitionDisk` step therefore fails for every such `Restart`. A `Restart` with `run_in` `Either` passes. The agent fails it at run time if the state is still on `X:`.
- A `Restart` step with `into` `WinPe` that comes after an `EnableBitLocker` step in program order. After the partition step, the state lives on the Windows volume. Once BitLocker protects that volume, the agent in WinPE cannot read the state there (inferred). See [BitLocker](#bitlocker).
- An `ApplyDrivers` or `ApplyUnattend` step that comes before the first `ApplyOsImage` step in program order. Both actions act on the applied image. See [Injection and verification](#injection-and-verification) and [Answer files](#answer-files). A sequence with no `ApplyOsImage` step therefore fails for every such step. The rule ignores conditions, so an `ApplyOsImage` step in a group with a condition still comes before (owner decision on [#180](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/180#issuecomment-6961336564)). The agent fails the later step at run time if the image step was skipped. MDT's `Client.xml` stages drivers and fills in its answer file in Preinstall, before the image, and `LTIApply.wsf` applies both after it (`Templates/Client.xml:269-281`, `scripts/LTIApply.wsf:915-926`, `scripts/LTIApply.wsf:1011-1046`, relative to `Source/MDT.8456` in the [MDT 8456 sources](research/mdt-8456.md#sources), and the MDT 8456 reference on [drivers](research/mdt-8456.md#drivers) and [answer files](research/mdt-8456.md#unattend)). So an MDT importer moves these steps after the image.
- Two nodes in one tree that share a `NodeId`. `Instr` and the errors identify a node by its ID, so an ID must be unique.

| Action | Allowed `run_in` |
| --- | --- |
| `PartitionDisk`, `ApplyOsImage`, `ApplyDrivers`, `ApplyUnattend`, `CheckReadiness` | `WinPe` |
| `InstallApplications`, `InstallUpdates`, `VerifyDrivers`, `ActivateWindows` | `FullOs` |
| `RunCommand`, `RunPowerShell`, `SetVariable`, `Restart`, `ServerCall`, `AddProvisioningPackage` | `WinPe`, `FullOs`, or `Either` |
| `EnableBitLocker`, `SuspendBitLocker` | `FullOs` |

- `BuiltinAction::allowed_run_in` holds the table as one exhaustive match, so a new action does not compile until someone decides where it runs.
- `plan` and `validate` return every error in document order, not only the first.
- Each error names the node's ID, its path in the authored sequence, such as `root.children[1].group.children[0]`, and the rule. An ID that two nodes share cannot locate either node, so the duplicate error also names the path of the first node with that ID.
- An error inside an expansion names the add-on step or join slot, its path, and the child's index in the expansion, such as `root.children[1].expansion[0]`. The ID that `plan` derives for the child never reaches the admin (owner decision on [#285](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/285#issuecomment-6988668500)).
- A disabled node is still part of the sequence that you save, so the `run_in` rule, the WinPE condition rule, and the duplicate ID rule check it. The two `Restart` rules read program order, so they skip disabled nodes (design decision). The rule for `ApplyDrivers` and `ApplyUnattend` also reads program order, so it skips them too (owner decision on [#180](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/180#issuecomment-6961336564)). The rule for an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition at or before the first `Restart` into `InstalledOs` also reads program order, so it skips them too, and a disabled `Restart` does not count (owner decision on [#160](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/160#issuecomment-6961338417)).

`validate` runs these rules when you save any authored sequence (owner decision on [#188](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/188#issuecomment-6961448743)). `validate`, `plan`, and `deferred` live in one public `plan` module, and `deployarr-core` has no public `lower` or `compile`, so only `plan` builds a `Program` (owner decision on [#285](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/285#issuecomment-6988668500)). Both functions run the same two passes, so each rule is written once:

1. The substitution pass builds an internal tree from the authored sequence. At planning, each deferred step becomes its expansion. At save, each one becomes an unexpanded deferred step, because the expansion is not known until planning. The step's ID and condition still take part in the rules. The pass also runs the three rules about add-on steps, join slots, and the actions that only an expansion may hold, which [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable) lists. At save, it checks each add-on step against its kind. At planning, it checks each expansion.
2. The rule pass checks the rules above over that tree and builds the program. `plan` runs it only when the substitution pass finds nothing and every expansion is used. A broken tree would give the rule pass nothing sound to check.

An unexpanded deferred step may hold any action whose `ActionKind` an add-on may return. The rule pass tracks each fact about program order as no, maybe, or yes: a `PartitionDisk` step so far, an `ApplyOsImage` step, an `EnableBitLocker` step, and a `Restart` into `InstalledOs`. At an unexpanded deferred step that the program keeps, each fact that an action from an add-on could make true becomes maybe, unless it is already yes. A rule about program order is reported only when every possible expansion would break it (owner decision on [#285](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/285#issuecomment-6988668500)). With the add-on action list of level 1, this suspends one rule: the rule on an `InstalledMsi` or `Wmi` condition at or before the first `Restart` into `InstalledOs`, at a node after the first unexpanded deferred step that the program keeps. That is the #160 decision, and it follows from the action list (owner decision on [#160](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/160#issuecomment-6981685250)). The step's own condition comes before its expansion, so the rule still checks it and every node before it. Planning checks the rest, so a save never rejects what planning accepts. Every rule that `validate` reports also fails `plan`, whatever the expansions. A rule that `plan` gains later reaches the save with no change to `validate`. Allowing a new action in expansions changes what an unexpanded deferred step may hold, with no change to the rule pass. See [Task sequences stay loadable](#task-sequences-stay-loadable).

The parser rejects other mistakes before the compiler runs, such as an empty success list or an empty `All` or `Any`. See [JSON format](#json-format).

### Conditions

- The agent evaluates a condition when it reaches the group or step. A false group condition skips the group. A false step condition skips the step and leaves `last` unchanged, as MDT does.
- The agent expands `Template` fields in a condition when it evaluates it, not when the server compiles the sequence.
- Evaluation returns `Result<bool, ConditionError>`. An error is not false. It fails the step and goes through the step's `on_error`, which is MDT's rule.
- A group whose condition fails to evaluate fails as a group. The run follows the `on_error` of the group's `Enter`. It goes where a failed step without `continue_on_error` directly inside the group would go (see [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program)). A group with `continue_on_error` jumps past its last child. A group without it passes the failure up, to the end of the nearest enclosing group with `continue_on_error`. With no such group, the deployment fails. The root follows the same rule. The error leaves `last` unchanged. MDT's engine does the same. For a group, it sets neither `_SMSTSLastActionRetCode` nor `_SMSTSLastActionSucceeded` ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). The owner chose this rule on [#150](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/150).
- `InstalledMsi` and `Wmi` work only in the full OS. The compiler rejects a group or an `Either` step with one that comes at or before the first `Restart` into `InstalledOs` (see [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program)). After that `Restart`, the run can still reach such a condition in WinPE, such as after a `Restart` into `WinPe`. There it fails to evaluate, and the rules above for a condition that fails to evaluate apply.
- The agent runs a `Wmi` condition's query exactly as substitution leaves it. It changes neither the case of the text nor any other part of it, such as the spaces at either end. This departs from MDT, which upper-cases the expanded query before it runs it ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). That MDT upper-cases the query is inferred, because `CCM::Utility::String::toUpper`, which MDT calls on the query, is in `CcmCore.dll`. The case of a constant in a WHERE clause comparison test does not change the test's answer, because "Comparison tests are always case-insensitive" ([WHERE Clause](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/wmisdk/where-clause)). An MDT importer copies a WMI condition's query text unchanged. The owner chose the agent's rule ([#279](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/279#issuecomment-6988083229)) and the importer's rule ([#279](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/279#issuecomment-6989846105)).
- In the MDT importer's rules in this section, white space is U+0020 space, U+0009 tab, U+000D carriage return, and U+000A line feed, and no other character. A trim at an end removes these characters from that end up to the first character outside the set. Text made only of these characters is empty after the trim. The importer keeps every other character at either end, including other Unicode white space such as U+00A0. Rust's `str::trim` removes every Unicode white space character, and `char::is_ascii_whitespace` also matches U+000C, so neither gives this set. The owner chose this set on [#335](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/335#issuecomment-7016379795).
  - It is the set that MDT's `CCM::Utility::String::trimLeft` and `trimRight` remove. Both keep every other character, such as U+00A0 ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#ccmcoredll-string-helpers)).
  - It is also the white space of XML 1.0 ([`S`](https://www.w3.org/TR/xml/#NT-S)). MDT's parser reads condition text through MSXML's `text` property, which trims white space at both ends ([text property](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ms762687%28v=vs.85%29), [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). Microsoft's page names no set. That `text` trims this set is inferred from XML 1.0's white space, because MSXML is outside the binary. U+000B and U+000C cannot occur in XML 1.0 text, because the `Char` production excludes them ([Char](https://www.w3.org/TR/xml/#NT-Char)).
- The agent passes a `Wmi` condition's namespace to the probe as written and does not substitute it. The namespace is a plain `String`, not a `Template` (see [Core data shapes](#core-data-shapes)). This departs from MDT, which expands the namespace when the condition runs, before it opens it (`TsCore!0x1801aa655` in the [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). The agent opens a `Wmi` condition's namespace exactly as written, with no trimming, just as it runs the query exactly as substitution leaves it. This also departs from MDT, which trims the namespace. MDT's condition parser reads the text of each `<variable>` element, including the one that holds the namespace, through MSXML's `text` property when it parses the sequence (`TsCore!0x180165124`, called at `TsCore!0x18019a259`, with the `get_text` call at `TsCore!0x180165169`). Microsoft documents that `text` "trims the whitespace on the edges of the result" ([text property](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ms762687%28v=vs.85%29)). That MDT therefore trims the namespace at both ends before it expands it is inferred from that page, because MSXML is outside the binary ([#336](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/336)). After it expands the namespace, MDT calls `CCM::Utility::String::trimLeft` on it before it opens it (`TsCore!0x1801aa762` and `TsCore!0x1801aa941` in the [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). MDT calls no `trimRight` on the expanded namespace (`TsCore!0x1801aa380`). `trimLeft` removes white space, as defined above, from the start of the namespace ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#ccmcoredll-string-helpers)). An MDT importer trims white space, as defined above, at both ends of a WMI condition's namespace. It changes nothing else in the namespace. An imported namespace without `%` that is not empty after the trim is therefore the string that MDT passes to `CCM::Utility::Wmi::Namespace::Open` when it reaches that call (`TsCore!0x1801aa941`). This match is inferred too, because it depends on what MSXML's `text` property removes or changes. The importer trims a namespace that holds `%` the same way, and adds a note that names the condition. Such a namespace can still differ from the string that MDT passes to `Namespace::Open`, because MDT expands it and then trims its start again, and the agent does not substitute it. The owner chose the substitution rule and the note on [#301](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/301#issuecomment-6992982144), and the trim rules on [#300](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/300#issuecomment-6992982890). The owner later changed the importer's trim from the start only to both ends ([#300](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/300#issuecomment-7001536754)).
- `Var` compares as integers when both sides are integers, and otherwise compares strings without regard to case, like MDT. `Like` matches `*` and `?` wildcards. `Exists` means the variable is set and not empty. A `Registry` comparison applies the `Var` rules to the text of both sides, with a number as its decimal text.
- An integer is an optional `-` followed by one or more ASCII digits. Empty text and a lone `-` are not integers, so they compare as text. An unset variable reads as empty text, so it compares as text too. This departs from MDT, whose integer test passes empty text. MDT therefore compares an empty or unset variable with an integer Value as an integer ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). That MDT reads the empty text as 0 is inferred, because `CCM::Utility::String::toInt64`, which reads it, is in `CcmCore.dll`.
- A `Registry` `Like` test matches the pattern against the value's text, as a `Var` `Like` test does. This departs from MDT, whose registry code has no Like branch. MDT's registry Like is true for every `REG_DWORD`, `REG_SZ`, or `REG_EXPAND_SZ` value that exists, whatever the pattern ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)).
- A `Registry` condition with a value type compares the type of the value that it finds with the value type before it applies the test. A value of another type counts as missing for every test. `Exists` is false, `NotExists` is true, and each comparison and `Like` is false, never an error. MDT does the same when its `Type` parameter and a value name are both set ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). A value that the probe reads as `RegValue::Other` matches by its type number. A value of the named type follows the rules above, so a comparison on a `REG_BINARY` value with a value type of `REG_BINARY` still fails to evaluate. A missing key, a missing value, and a probe that cannot answer give the same result with a value type as without one. Without a value type, a test reads a value of any type. The owner chose this rule on [#277](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/277#issuecomment-6988081213).
- A `Var` Value that expands to empty text when the condition runs makes a comparison fail to evaluate. `Like` against empty text is not an error. This follows MDT, which checks for an empty Value after it expands the Value, and only for the six operators from Equals to GreaterEqual ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). The parser already rejects a Value that is empty before expansion (see [Parameter types](#parameter-types)).
- A comparison or `Like` test on a list value is an error, the same way a list in a `Template` fails the step (see [Variables](#variables)). `Exists` and `NotExists` work on a list, and a list with at least one item exists. A test on list items needs its own operator, which a later ticket can add (owner decision on [#177](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/177)).
- A missing file, folder, registry key, or registry value makes the condition false. A file or folder that the agent cannot read makes the condition fail to evaluate, not false. This departs from MDT, whose `File::Exists` and `Directory::Exists` return only true or false, so MDT cannot tell a missing path from one it cannot read (inferred in the [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions), because both functions are in `CcmCore.dll`).
- `Not` takes one child. MDT's `not` with several children means "none of them is true", so an MDT importer turns it into `Not(Any(children))`.
- The MDT importer replaces a Variable or Registry test whose operator is AnyVersion or ThisVersion, because `Test` has neither operator. MDT matches both names in any letter case (`TsCore!0x1801ab0b0`), and so does the importer. MDT's editor writes neither operator on these two conditions, so a sequence holds one only when something other than the editor wrote it ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#task-sequence-editor-model)). The owner chose these rules on [#278](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/278#issuecomment-6988082376):
  - MDT's Variable test with either operator is true whenever its evaluation reaches the comparison. Every earlier exit is an error, so the test is never false (`TsCore!0x1801a96c0`, `TsCore!0x1801ab054`). The importer turns such a test on variable `x` into `Any([Var{x, Exists}, Var{x, NotExists}])`. `Exists` is true when `x` is set and not empty, and `NotExists` is true in every other case, so the `Any` is true on every run. Neither test takes an operand or reads the machine, so the `Any` never fails to evaluate. It gives MDT's true result inside `All`, `Any`, and `Not` without a new `Condition` variant.
  - In these rules, `Type` counts as set when its expanded text is not empty after the trim, as in the `Type` rules below. MDT's Registry test with either operator and a value name is true for a `REG_DWORD`, `REG_SZ`, or `REG_EXPAND_SZ` value that exists. It is false when the key or the value is missing. It is also false when `Type` is set and the value has another type, because MDT then treats the value as missing. It fails to evaluate on a value of a type other than those three, such as `REG_BINARY`, when `Type` is not set or maps to that type ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). With no value name, the test is false when the key is missing. When the key exists, it fails to evaluate, because MDT reads no value type there (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`, the value name test at `0x1801a78d3` and the type check at `0x1801a7c89`).
  - The importer turns a Registry test with either operator and a value name into a `Registry` test with `Exists` on the same key, the same value name, and the same value type. The `Type` rules below give the value type. A test with no value name becomes `Exists` on the key, with no value type.
  - The `Exists` test matches MDT's result for a missing key or value, for a `REG_DWORD`, `REG_SZ`, or `REG_EXPAND_SZ` value whose `Type` holds no `%VAR%` reference, and for a value whose type differs from a `Type` that the importer copies into the value type. It departs from MDT in two cases. In the first, MDT fails to evaluate and `Exists` is true. That is a value of a type other than those three, such as `REG_BINARY`, when `Type` is not set, or holds a `%VAR%` reference that maps to that type when the condition runs. The second, in the next item, comes from a `Type` with a `%VAR%` reference. There MDT is false and `Exists` is true. Two cases are not such departures, because the importer disables their nodes, as the last two items below say. They are a test with no value name on a key that exists, and a value of the type that a fixed `Type` outside those three maps to.
  - A `Type` with a `%VAR%` reference gives no value type, by the `Type` rules below. For a value whose type differs from the type that the expanded `Type` maps to, `Exists` is then true and MDT is false ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)).
  - The importer adds a note for each test that it replaces, on the step or group whose condition held the test. The note names the MDT operator and the names in the test. Those are the variable name for a Variable test, and the key and the value name for a Registry test. A Registry test's `Value` parameter holds the value name (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`), so the note names it. The note holds no values. It leaves out a Variable test's `Value` and a Registry test's `Data`, as the notes in [Core data shapes](#core-data-shapes) do.
  - For a Registry test with either operator and no value name, the importer still writes `Exists` on the key, with no value type. It also sets `enabled` to false on the step or group whose condition holds the test, at any depth inside `All`, `Any`, or `Not`. It keeps the node's whole condition, so an admin can edit it and turn the node back on. The node keeps its place, and the kept `Registry` condition breaks no `validate` rule. A disabled group takes its children with it, because the compiler drops disabled nodes (see [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program)). Disabling a node can still make `validate` fail a later node. The rule below for such a later node covers that case. The note from the item above also says that the importer disabled the node, and why. It still holds no values. The owner chose this rule on [#320](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/320#issuecomment-7001426939).
    - The reason is that MDT never gives this test true. On a key that exists, MDT fails to evaluate it. On a missing key, MDT gives false (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`, the missing-key path at `0x1801a77ac`, `0x1801a7806`, and `0x1801a7c5d`). That a missing key takes this path is inferred from the log text `Registry key %s does not exist` at `TsCore!0x1801a77f2` ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). The importer sees the test when it imports the sequence, so the draft shows the problem before any PC runs it.
    - deployarr skips the disabled node on every PC. For a condition that holds only this test, a missing key gives the same result as in MDT, where the test is false and the step or group is skipped. On a key that exists, MDT fails the step or group. A failure that no `continueOnError` catches stops the run (`TsManager!0x140033c10`, `TsManager!0x140033664`, [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#instruction-table-and-failure-handling)). deployarr skips the node there, and the run goes on.
    - Under an `or` or a `not`, MDT can still run the step or group on some PCs. MDT's `and`, `or`, and `not` stop at the first child that settles the answer, or at the first child that fails to evaluate (`TsCore!0x1801a0dc0`, `TsCore!0x1801a55e8`, `TsCore!0x1801a53e4`). Two examples are `not` on a missing key and `or` with a true sibling. deployarr never runs the disabled node.
    - The same condition can also hold an operator that the importer cannot convert, by the rule for operator text that the owner chose on [#312](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/312#issuecomment-6993283308). That rule then applies, and the importer leaves out the whole condition. The note gives both reasons.
  - For a Registry test with either operator, a value name, and a fixed `Type` that maps to `REG_BINARY`, `REG_MULTI_SZ`, `REG_QWORD`, or `REG_NONE`, the importer still writes `Exists` on the same key, the same value name, and that value type, which the `Type` rules below give. A fixed `Type` holds no `%VAR%` reference and is not empty after the trim. `Type` text that matches none of the seven names counts, because it maps to `REG_NONE`. The importer also sets `enabled` to false on the step or group whose condition holds the test, at any depth inside `All`, `Any`, or `Not`. It keeps the node's whole condition, so an admin can edit it and turn the node back on. The node keeps its place, and the kept `Registry` condition breaks no `validate` rule. A disabled group takes its children with it, because the compiler drops disabled nodes (see [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program)). Disabling a node can still make `validate` fail a later node. The rule below for such a later node covers that case. The note that the importer adds for the replaced test also says that the importer disabled the node, and why, and names the value type. It still holds no values. When the `Type` text matched no name, the note from the `Type` rules below still applies. The owner chose this rule on [#338](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/338#issuecomment-7002517642).
    - The reason is that MDT never gives this test true. A missing key makes it false (`TsCore!0x1801a77ac`, `TsCore!0x1801a7806`, `TsCore!0x1801a7c5d`). That a missing key takes this path is inferred from the log text `Registry key %s does not exist` at `TsCore!0x1801a77f2`. A missing value makes it false (`TsCore!0x1801a78ff`, `TsCore!0x1801a7aef` to `TsCore!0x1801a7b64`, `TsCore!0x1801a7806`, `TsCore!0x1801a7c5d`). That `CCM::Utility::RegKey::ValueExists`, called at `TsCore!0x1801a78ff`, reports a missing value on this path is inferred from the log text `Registry value %s\%s does not exist`, because the function is in `CcmCore.dll`. A value of another type counts as missing, so the test is false (`TsCore!0x1801a7a2e` to `TsCore!0x1801a7a56`, `TsCore!0x1801a7aea`). A value of exactly that type fails to evaluate. The type limit admits only `REG_DWORD`, `REG_SZ`, and `REG_EXPAND_SZ` for every operator but Exists and NotExists, so the engine fails the condition with `0x80004005` (`TsCore!0x1801a7c48`, `TsCore!0x1801a7c89` to `TsCore!0x1801a7ca8`, `TsCore!0x1801a8454`, [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)). The `Type` is fixed, so the importer sees the value type when it imports the sequence, and the draft shows the problem before any PC runs it.
    - deployarr skips the disabled node on every PC. For a condition that holds only this test, or holds it under an `and`, MDT never runs the step or group either. A missing key, a missing value, and a value of another type give the same result as in MDT, where the step or group is skipped. On a value of that type, MDT fails the step or group. A failure that no `continueOnError` catches stops the run (`TsManager!0x140033c10`, `TsManager!0x140033664`, [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#instruction-table-and-failure-handling)). deployarr skips the node there, and the run goes on.
    - Under an `or` or a `not`, MDT can still run the step or group on some PCs. MDT's `and`, `or`, and `not` stop at the first child that settles the answer, or at the first child that fails to evaluate (`TsCore!0x1801a0dc0`, `TsCore!0x1801a55e8`, `TsCore!0x1801a53e4`). Examples are `not` on a missing key, a missing value, or a value of another type, and `or` with a true sibling. deployarr never runs the disabled node.
    - The same condition can also hold a test with no value name that the item above or the Like rule below disables. The importer then disables the node once, and the note gives each reason.
    - The same condition can also hold an operator that the importer cannot convert, by the rule for operator text that the owner chose on [#312](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/312#issuecomment-6993283308). That rule then applies, and the importer leaves out the whole condition. The note gives both reasons.
- The MDT importer converts a Registry test that names no value and has Like, or one of the six comparisons from Equals to GreaterEqual, by these rules. The owner chose them on [#331](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/331#issuecomment-7002491396). deployarr cannot hold either test as it is. `RegistryCondition::new` rejects a test other than `Exists` or `NotExists` that names no value, and `RegValueName` rejects empty text (see [Parameter types](#parameter-types)). The rules apply once the operator text rules below have matched the operator, whatever the test's `Data`. MDT's editor offers no Like on a Registry condition, so a sequence holds a Registry Like only when something other than the editor wrote it ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#task-sequence-editor-model)).
  - MDT never gives either test true. MDT reports a Registry comparison with an empty value name as an error, and the check covers only the six operators from Equals to GreaterEqual (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`, the check at `0x1801a760d`). The [MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions) records no case where such a test is true or false. MDT's registry code has no Like branch. It gives Like the results that it gives AnyVersion and ThisVersion, with a value name and without one (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`, `TsCore!0x1801ab054`). So with an empty value name, Like is an error on a key that exists, because MDT reads no value type and the type check fails. On a missing key, Like is false (`TsCore!0x1801a6bec`). That a missing key takes this path is inferred from the log text `Registry key %s does not exist` at `TsCore!0x1801a77f2` ([MDT 8450 binaries reference](research/mdt-8450-binaries.md#conditions)).
  - For a Like test, the importer does what the AnyVersion and ThisVersion rule above does for a test with no value name, which the owner chose on [#320](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/320#issuecomment-7001426939). It turns the test into `Exists` on the key, with no value type. It drops `Type` with no note, as the `Type` rules below do for a test of the key. It sets `enabled` to false on the step or group whose condition holds the test, at any depth inside `All`, `Any`, or `Not`. It keeps that node's whole condition, so an admin can edit it and turn the node back on. Like keeps its converted condition, because MDT gives Like with no value name the same results as AnyVersion and ThisVersion with no value name.
  - For a comparison, the importer does what the operator text rules below do for an operator that it cannot convert, which the owner chose on [#312](https://github.com/Tsukidy/deployarr/issues/312#issuecomment-6993283308). It sets `enabled` to false on the step or group whose condition holds the test, at any depth inside `All`, `Any`, or `Not`. It leaves out that node's whole condition. `RegistryCondition` cannot hold a comparison without a value name, and a partial condition could change the condition's meaning, for example inside `Not`. MDT gives the test no true or false result, so no deployarr test can stand in for it.
  - The node keeps its place in the sequence. A disabled group takes its children with it, because the compiler drops disabled nodes (see [Compile the tree into a flat program](#compile-the-tree-into-a-flat-program)). Disabling a node can make `validate` fail a later node. The rule below for such a later node covers that case.
  - The importer adds a note on that node. The note names the MDT operator and the key, and says that the test names no value. It says that the importer disabled the node, and why. For a comparison, it also says that the importer left out the node's condition. The note holds no values, so it leaves out the test's `Data`, as the notes in [Core data shapes](#core-data-shapes) do.
  - One node's condition can hold a test that the Like rule or the AnyVersion and ThisVersion rule converts, and also a test that the comparison rule or the operator text rules leave out. Then the importer leaves out the whole condition, and the note gives each reason.
  - The reason for disabling the node is that MDT never gives either test true, so a disabled node never runs on a PC where MDT would not run it. The importer sees the test when it imports the sequence, so the draft shows the problem before any PC runs it.
  - deployarr skips the disabled node on every PC, on a missing key and on a key that exists. For a condition that holds only a Like test, a missing key gives the same result as in MDT, where the test is false and the step or group is skipped. On a key that exists, MDT fails the Like test. MDT most likely fails a comparison on a missing key and on a key that exists alike (inferred, because the reference records the error with no condition on the key).
